## 5<sub>.</sub>4 T<sub>as</sub>k f<sub>ram</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>

Havin<sub>g</sub> established that state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g</sub> is <sub>g</sub>overnance-sha<sub>p</sub>ed rather than <sub>g</sub>eneric<sub>,</sub> we turn to the in<sub>p</sub>ut conditions under which it occ<sub>u</sub>rs<sub>,</sub> examinin<sub>g</sub> <sub>v</sub>ariation across the elicitation <sub>p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m and the level of visual evidence. The elicitation <sub>p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m stron<sub>g</sub>l<sub>y</sub> afects how often state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g</sub> occurs (Table 2). The text-onl<sub>y</sub> condition (comment-text), in which the subject is named in text <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s as</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub> it<sub>, pro</sub>d<sub>uces s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e-a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d f<sub>ram-</sub> in<sub>g</sub> in 36.5% of trials and ex<sub>p</sub>licit refusal in 25.0%. The contrast is <sup>d</sup>riven <sup>b</sup>y <sup>h</sup>ow t<sup>h</sup>e su<sup>b</sup>ject is <sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup>ivere<sup>d</sup>: w<sup>h</sup>en t<sup>h</sup>e same su<sup>b</sup>ject is <sub>p</sub>resented as an ima<sub>g</sub>e (comment-image) rather than named in text<sub>,</sub> the framin<sub>g</sub> rate is onl<sub>y</sub> 9.8%<sub>,</sub> statisticall<sub>y</sub> indistin<sub>g</sub>uishable from describe (OR 1.13, n.s.; Table A4). Namin<sub>g</sub> the subject in text (comment-text) instead <sub>p</sub>roduces rou<sub>g</sub>hl<sub>y</sub> four times the framin<sub>g</sub> rate of the ima<sub>g</sub>e condition (36.5% vs. 9.8%; text-vs-ima<sub>g</sub>e OR = 6.27 in t<sup>h</sup>e joint mo<sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup>). Framing is t<sup>h</sup>us <sup>d</sup>riven <sup>b</sup>y text-<sup>b</sup>ase<sup>d</sup> su<sup>b</sup>ject deliver<sub>y</sub> rather than ima<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>resentation. Visual abstraction reduces framin<sub>g</sub> relative to describe $\mathrm { ( O R = 0 . 2 3 }$ ; Table A4), with the abstracted variants (A1–A6) <sub>p</sub>roducin<sub>g</sub> less framin<sub>g</sub> than the ori<sub>g</sub>inal (A0) (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 5a).

Across the 5,292 abstraction trials (Table A8), the state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> 3<sub>.</sub>4% <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> 2<sub>.</sub>4% <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>ilh<sub>ou-</sub> <sub>e</sub>tt<sub>e</sub> <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t b<sub>u</sub>t d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t d<sub>ec</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>mono</sub>t<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> (Jonckheere–Ter<sub>p</sub>stra � = 0.58; Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 5a). ## D Multi-Rater Judge Validation

T<sup>h</sup>is appen<sup>d</sup>ix provi<sup>d</sup>es t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> resu<sup>l</sup>ts o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e mu<sup>l</sup>ti-rater va<sup>l</sup>i<sup>d</sup>ation <sup>d</sup>escri<sup>b</sup>e<sup>d</sup> in section 4.3. First, agreement <sup>b</sup>etween t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>h</sup>uman-majority labels (Table A5) and both LLM jud<sub>g</sub>es is hi<sub>g</sub>h across dimensions, includin<sub>g</sub> the state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g</sub> dimension (D4). Second, residual disa reement on D4 is redominantl one-directional: humans label more res onses as state-ali ned than either jud e (Fi ure A1). This asymmetry imp<sup>l</sup>ies t<sup>h</sup>at t<sup>h</sup>e ju<sup>d</sup>ges un<sup>d</sup>er-<sup>l</sup>a<sup>b</sup>e<sup>l</sup> state-a<sup>l</sup>igne<sup>d</sup> <sup>f</sup>raming, motivating our interpretation o<sup>f</sup> reporte<sup>d</sup> preva<sup>l</sup>ence estimates as conservative lower bounds. For both jud<sub>g</sub>es false ne<sub>g</sub>atives (O<sub>p</sub>us 23, GPT 22) dominate false <sub>p</sub>ositives (O<sub>p</sub>us 3, GPT 1), so the residual <sup>d</sup>isagreement is overw<sup>h</sup>e<sup>l</sup>ming<sup>l</sup>y t<sup>h</sup>e ju<sup>d</sup>ge <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>ing to <sup>fl</sup>ag a <sup>h</sup>uman-i<sup>d</sup>enti<sup>fi</sup>e<sup>d</sup> positive rat<sup>h</sup>er t<sup>h</sup>an over-ca<sup>ll</sup>ing

Opus 4.7 was se<sup>l</sup>ected as t<sup>h</sup>e primary judge w<sup>h</sup>en t<sup>h</sup>e audit pipe<sup>l</sup>ine was <sup>l</sup>oc<sup>k</sup>ed, be<sup>f</sup>ore t<sup>h</sup>e va<sup>l</sup>idation samp<sup>l</sup>e was score<sup>d</sup>. ## G Judge Attenuation and Odds-Ratio Robustness

Let �<sup>ˆ</sup> be the jud<sub>g</sub>e’s label. Our multi-rater validation (section 4.3) established two facts: the jud<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>roduced ver<sub>y</sub> few false <sub>p</sub>ositives (O<sub>p</sub>us 3/200, GPT-5.5 1/200; the residual disagreement is overwhelmingly the human majority labeling more trials state-aligned), so specificity $\sigma = \mathrm { P r } ( \hat { Y } { = } 0 \mid Y { = } 0 ) \approx 1 ;$ <sub>a</sub>nd it mi<sub>sse</sub>d <sub>su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>nti<sub>a</sub>ll m<sub>o</sub>r<sub>e</sub> tr<sub>ue os</sub>iti<sub>ves, w</sub>ith <sub>se</sub>n<sub>s</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it $s = \mathrm { P r } ( \hat { Y } { = } 1 \mid Y { = } 1 ) < 1$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n any</sub> <sub>ce</sub>ll <sub>w</sub>ith t<sub>rue ra</sub>t<sub>e �</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
\hat { p } = \operatorname* { P r } ( \hat { Y } { = } 1 ) = s \pi + ( 1 - \sigma ) ( 1 - \pi ) \approx s \pi \leq \pi ,\tag{3}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se-pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>erm</sub> $\bigl ( 1 - \sigma \bigr ) \bigl ( 1 - \pi \bigr )$ is ne<sub>g</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ible $( 1 - \sigma \leq 1 . 5 \% )$ and is dominated b<sub>y</sub> the sensitivit<sub>y</sub> loss<sub>;</sub> to this a<sub>pp</sub>roximation all <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es are a</sub> l<sub>ower</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> t<sub>wo ce</sub>ll<sub>s w</sub>ith t<sub>rue ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> $\pi _ { 1 } , \pi _ { 2 }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>sens</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y �,</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>o</sub>dd<sub>s ra</sub>ti<sub>o</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
{ \widehat { \mathrm { O R } } } = { \frac { s \pi _ { 1 } / ( 1 - s \pi _ { 1 } ) } { s \pi _ { 2 } / ( 1 - s \pi _ { 2 } ) } } .\tag{4}
$$

In the rare-o<sub>u</sub>tcome re<sub>g</sub>ime $( \pi _ { j }$ <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>,</sub> <sub>so</sub> $1 - s \pi _ { j } \approx 1$ <sub>an</sub>d $1 - \pi _ { j } \approx 1 )$ ,

$$
\widehat { \mathrm { O R } } \approx \frac { s \pi _ { 1 } } { s \pi _ { 2 } } = \frac { \pi _ { 1 } } { \pi _ { 2 } } \approx \mathrm { O R } _ { \mathrm { t r u e } } ,\tag{5}
$$

because the unknown sensitivit<sub>y</sub> � cancels. Hence the jud<sub>g</sub>e’s incom<sub>p</sub>leteness attenuates the levels (b<sub>y</sub> the factor �) but leaves the odds ratios a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> unbiased. This is wh<sub>y</sub> we re<sub>p</sub>ort efects as odds ratios from E<sub>q</sub>. (1): the<sub>y</sub> are the <sub>q</sub>uantities robust to the dominant im<sub>p</sub>erfection our validation detected (incom<sub>p</sub>lete sensitivit<sub>y</sub>). The same ar<sub>g</sub>ument shows the lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ate <sub>�</sub> and ori<sub>g</sub>in term � are estimated consistentl<sub>y</sub> u<sub>p</sub> to the ne<sub>g</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ible �(�) correction, while the absolute 10.91% <sub>p</sub>revalence should be read as “at least 10.91%.”

## <sup>H</sup>.<sup>2</sup> <sup>L</sup>anguage-ga<sup>t</sup>e <sup>di</sup>agnos<sup>ti</sup>cs

The Chinese-lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ate re<sub>p</sub>orted in the main text is not an a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ate artifact: it holds within each ori<sub>g</sub>in <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> and in ever<sub>y</sub> individual mo<sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup>. ## I Prompt-sensitivity ablation (describe vs. narrative): state-aligned framing

Motivation and <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts. Our headline measurements use a sin<sub>g</sub>le neutral describe <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t (“Please describe the content of this <sub>p</sub>hoto<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h”), which <sub>p</sub>robes an ima<sub>g</sub>e’s surface and lets a model list visual elements without inter<sub>p</sub>retin<sub>g</sub> the de<sub>p</sub>icted event. To test whether this conservative <sub>p</sub>hrasin<sub>g</sub> su<sub>pp</sub>resses state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g,</sub> we add one further elicitation <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t that moves from surface descri<sub>p</sub>tion toward event inter<sub>p</sub>retation while introducin<sub>g</sub> no named entities<sub>,</sub> locations<sub>,</sub> or contextual cues. We ado<sub>p</sub>t the o<sub>p</sub>en-ended narrative <sub>q</sub>uestion “What is ha<sub>pp</sub>enin<sub>g</sub> in this ima<sub>g</sub>e?”, and its Chinese counter<sub>p</sub>art, followin<sub>g</sub> THRONE’s free-form VLM evaluation st<sub>y</sub>le [24]. The narrative <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t names no subject and contains none of the <sub>p</sub>oliticall<sub>y</sub> loaded vocabular<sub>y</sub> (event, back<sub>g</sub>round, historical, <sub>p</sub>olitical, . . . ) that could leak <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>;</sub> it i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre-spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> <sub>max</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Design. The narrative <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t is run on the same 200 core ima<sub>g</sub>es, all nine models, both lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>es, and the same three seeds (42, 622, 997) as the describe baseline, under identical sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> (temperature=0.7, top\_p=0.8, top\_k=20, max\_tokens=1024; reasonin<sub>g</sub> disabled for the two reasonin<sub>g</sub>-ca<sub>p</sub>able check<sub>p</sub>oints), <sub>g</sub>ivin<sub>g</sub> $2 0 0 \times 9 \times 2 \times 3 = 1 0 , 8 0 0$ a<sub>pp</sub>endix-onl<sub>y</sub> trials. These trials are <sub>p</sub>aired to the main describe baseline for sensitivity analysis but are not added to the 21,708-trial headline corpus. Both judges (Claude Opus 4.7 and GPT-5.5, non-thinking re-audit the narrative res<sub>p</sub>onses under the same locked rubric, and the two <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t conditions are <sub>p</sub>aired 1:1 on (model, entr<sub>y</sub>, lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e, seed). We re<sub>p</sub>ort <sub>p</sub>aired McNemar tests on the cate<sub>g</sub>orical dimensions and a <sub>p</sub>aired Wilcoxon test on res<sub>p</sub>onse len<sub>g</sub>th.

Result. State-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g</sub> is hi<sub>g</sub>her under the narrative <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t than under describe for both jud<sub>g</sub>es (Table A9, Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A11): overal +2.6 <sub>p</sub>oints under O<sub>p</sub>us $4 . 7 \ : ( 8 . 8  1 1 . 4 \% , p \ : < \ : 1 0 ^ { - 1 9 } )$ and +5.4 <sub>p</sub>oints under $\mathrm { G P T - 5 . 5 } ( 1 1 . 5  1 6 . 9 \%   p < 1 0 ^ { - 5 7 } )$ . The increase is lar<sub>g</sub>est under Chinese-lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts and for China-ori<sub>g</sub>in models; the non-China shift is jud<sub>g</sub>e-de<sub>p</sub>endent (flat under O<sub>p</sub>us, +2.3 <sub>p</sub>oints under GPT-5.5). Per model, the lar<sub>g</sub>est describe-to-narrative shifts occur in the two newest Qwen <sub>g</sub>enerations (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A12), and the model-leve <sub>p</sub>aired test is si<sub>g</sub>nificant under GPT-5.5 (9/9 models) but not under O<sub>p</sub>us (6/9, Wilcoxon $p = 0 . 3 0 ) \mathrm { ; }$ <sub>; we</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore rea</sub>d th<sub>e e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>as ro</sub>b<sub>us</sub>t <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e r</sub>i<sub>se</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>a</sub> l<sub>onger-response ar</sub>tif<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>: mean</sub> l<sub>eng</sub>th f<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> 604 t<sub>o</sub> 556 <sub>c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>ers un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> the narrative <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t while framin<sub>g</sub> rises (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A13), and it <sub>p</sub>ersists when restricted to trials that are non-refusals under both <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts. The discourse-strate<sub>gy</sub> mix (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A15) and the full <sub>p</sub>er-dimension shift (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A14) match the main stud<sub>y</sub>: substitution remains the moda strate<sub>gy,</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>licit refusal rises sli<sub>g</sub>htl<sub>y,</sub> and information inte<sub>g</sub>rit<sub>y</sub> is not materiall<sub>y</sub> chan<sub>g</sub>ed. We therefore read describe as a conservative<sub>,</sub> prompt-suppresse<sup>d l</sup>ower <sup>b</sup>oun<sup>d</sup>, comp<sup>l</sup>ementing t<sup>h</sup>e ju<sup>d</sup>ge-attenuation <sup>l</sup>ower <sup>b</sup>oun<sup>d</sup> o<sup>f</sup> Appen<sup>d</sup>ix G. Two adjudicated trials. Two of the 10,800 narrative-<sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t trials (both Llama-3.2-11B-Vision, En<sub>g</sub>lish) re<sub>p</sub>eatedl<sub>y</sub> returned null content <sup>f</sup>rom t<sup>h</sup>e Opus ju<sup>d</sup>ge/proxy on <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup>-prompt ca<sup>ll</sup>s; we treate<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>ese as primary-ju<sup>d</sup>ge-unavai<sup>l</sup>a<sup>bl</sup>e an<sup>d</sup> use<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e GPT-5.5 <sup>l</sup>a<sup>b</sup>e<sup>l</sup> (<sup>b</sup>ot<sup>h</sup> non-state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned), which chan<sub>g</sub>es no re<sub>p</sub>orted rate b<sub>y</sub> more than 0.03 <sub>p</sub>oints.

## J Robustness: judges, units of analysis, and three-way verification

Every confirmatory claim was re-evaluated with two independent, non-thinking LLM judges run over the full 21,708-trial corpus (Claude O<sub>p</sub>us 4.7 and GPT-5.5) and the human-majorit<sub>y</sub> IRR set, under three inde<sub>p</sub>endent desi<sub>g</sub>ns: (i) fre<sub>q</sub>uentist NHST with Holm correction and model-clustered bootstra<sub>p</sub>s; (ii) <sub>p</sub>ermutation / null-model tests; and (iii) hierarchical Ba<sub>y</sub>esian mixed-efects models (random interce<sub>p</sub>ts for model and entry). We report an efect only when its direction agrees across all three; magnitudes that depend on the judge or fail the <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-as-un</sub>it t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>are</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

The origin efect. With each model as one observation (7 China, 2 non-China), the China:non-China SA risk ratio is 3.24× under O<sub>p</sub>us (Mann–Whitne<sub>y</sub> $ { p } = 0 . 0 2 8$ ; Ho<sup>l</sup>m-a<sup>d</sup>juste<sup>d</sup> $\begin{array} { r } { p = 0 . 1 1 ) } \end{array}$ , 1.61× under GPT-5.5 (� = 0.44), and 1.60× for the human majorit<sub>y</sub>. A 7-vs-2 s<sub>p</sub>lit ca<sub>p</sub>s t<sup>h</sup>e sma<sup>ll</sup>est attaina<sup>bl</sup>e two-si<sup>d</sup>e<sup>d</sup> permutation � at 0.056, so a mo<sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup>-<sup>l</sup>eve<sup>l f</sup>requentist rejection is unreac<sup>h</sup>a<sup>bl</sup>e in princip<sup>l</sup>e; a <sup>h</sup>ierarc<sup>h</sup>ica<sup>l</sup>

Ba esian estimate shrinks the ratio to 2.84× (95% CrI [0.68, 9.93], s annin 1). The direction (China > non-China) is stable across all three l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>sources.</sub>

W<sup>h</sup>y Opus is an upper <sup>b</sup>oun<sup>d</sup>. On t<sup>h</sup>e IRR set t<sup>h</sup>e Opus ju<sup>d</sup>ge misses state-a<sup>l</sup>igne<sup>d f</sup>raming in non-C<sup>h</sup>ina responses more o<sup>f</sup>ten than in China res<sub>p</sub>onses (6/6 vs. 17/35 missed; Fisher $p = 0 . 0 2 7 )$ <sub>,</sub> mechanicall<sub>y</sub> inflatin<sub>g</sub> the meas<sub>u</sub>red ori<sub>g</sub>in ratio<sub>;</sub> GPT-5.5’s miss rate is ori<sub>g</sub>in-s<sub>y</sub>mmetric (3/6 vs. 19/35). The O<sub>p</sub>us ratio is thus an u<sub>pp</sub>er bound and the cross-jud<sub>g</sub>e ran<sub>g</sub>e 1.6–3.2× brackets the efect.

## K Ima<sub>g</sub>e vs. text <sub>p</sub>aired <sub>p</sub>robe: all six dimensions

To pro<sup>b</sup>e w<sup>h</sup>et<sup>h</sup>er t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>l</sup>ower state-a<sup>l</sup>igne<sup>d f</sup>raming un<sup>d</sup>er comment-image re<sup>fl</sup>ects anc<sup>h</sup>oring or a <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>ure to recognize t<sup>h</sup>e su<sup>b</sup>ject, we report the full D1–D4 breakdown of the <sub>p</sub>aired <sub>p</sub>robe, not onl<sub>y</sub> framin<sub>g</sub> (Table A12), and s<sub>p</sub>lit the ima<sub>g</sub>e-arm framin<sub>g</sub> rate b<sub>y</sub> visual <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub> (Table A13). Lower framin<sub>g</sub> under ima<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>resentation is not accom<sub>p</sub>anied b<sub>y</sub> more refusal or hi<sub>g</sub>her inte<sub>g</sub>rit<sub>y</sub>, and <sub>g</sub>rounded ima<sub>g</sub>e res<sub>p</sub>onses reframe less than non-grounded ones under both judges. This is consistent with anchoring, but we cannot separate it from non-recognition: D3 captures scene-<sup>d</sup>escription consistency, not su<sup>b</sup>ject i<sup>d</sup>enti<sup>fi</sup>cation, so a groun<sup>d</sup>e<sup>d</sup> response may sti<sup>ll h</sup>ave <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> to recognize t<sup>h</sup>e po<sup>l</sup>itica<sup>l</sup> su<sup>b</sup>ject an<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>ere<sup>f</sup>ore <sup>h</sup>ave <sup>h</sup>a<sup>d</sup> not<sup>h</sup>ing to re<sup>f</sup>rame. In<sup>f</sup>ormation integrity stays <sup>l</sup>ow even among groun<sup>d</sup>e<sup>d</sup> responses, so groun<sup>d</sup>ing—w<sup>h</sup>atever its mechanism—reduces reframin<sub>g</sub> without restorin<sub>g</sub> omitted facts.

Table A12: Paired comment-image vs. comment-text <sub>p</sub>robe across all six audited dimensions<sub>,</sub> not onl<sub>y</sub> state-ali<sub>g</sub>ned framin<sub>g</sub>. Each condition is scored on ex<sub>p</sub>licit refusal (D1), information integrit<sub>y</sub> (D2, % true), visual grounding (D3, % grounded; N/A for the ima e-free text condition) and state-ali ned framin (D4) under both jud es over the full aired cor us. Lower D4 under comment-image is not accom<sub>p</sub>anied b<sub>y</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>her refusal or hi<sub>g</sub>her inte<sub>g</sub>rit<sub>y,</sub> which motivates the <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub>-conditioned analysis in Table A13. N/A denotes not applicable, not missing data: visual grounding (D3) measures whether a response’s image d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>on ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e p</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t<sub>ure, so</sub> it i<sub>s un</sub>d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mage-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> co<sub>mmen</sub>t<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on.</sub>

As a further probe of this omission, we isolate a selective-silence signature—grounded, non-refusal, non-framed responses that fail integrity b<sub>y</sub> omission—and test its content-selectivit<sub>y</sub> (Table A14). The rate is hi<sub>g</sub>her on sensitive than beni<sub>g</sub>n ima<sub>g</sub>es and concentrated in China-ori<sub>g</sub>in <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h b<sub>en</sub>i<sub>gn</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> it i<sub>s</sub> l<sub>arge</sub>l<sub>y a</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>ve om</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>on</sub> t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>ency ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an a c</sub>l<sub>ean censors</sub>hi<sub>p c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e-a</sub>li <sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>ram</sub>i<sub>n ; we</sub> fl<sub>a</sub> it <sub>as a</sub> t<sub>ar e</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> f<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ure wor</sub>k <sub>on</sub> f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er-sca</sub>l<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s.</sub>

The rate is higher on sensitive content and concentrated in China-origin models (ga<sub>p</sub> +11.6/+12.3 <sub>pp</sub>, OR ≈ 1.6), while non-China models show little selectivit<sub>y</sub> (e.g. +0.6 <sub>pp</sub> under O<sub>p</sub>us 4.7). This a<sub>pp</sub>endix s<sub>p</sub>ecifies the exact <sub>p</sub>rocedure used to com<sub>p</sub>ute the markdown-stri<sub>pp</sub>ed, scafold-stri<sub>pp</sub>ed character counts re<sub>p</sub>orted in §5 (the form-shift len<sub>g</sub>th anal<sub>y</sub>sis; footnote on Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A6).

## Motivation

The four Qwen multimodal generations difer sharply in rendering style: Qwen2-VL-7B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B emit flat paragraphs (> 98% of trials), while Qwen3-VL-8B and Qwen3.5-9B emit structured documents with bullets, bold <sup>“</sup>section labels,” horizontal rules, and a trailing “Summar<sub>y</sub>” meta-block in ∼ 63% of trials. Raw character counts therefore overestimate the substantive content of the newer two relative to the older two. To <sub>p</sub>ut the cross-<sub>g</sub>eneration len<sub>g</sub>th com<sub>p</sub>arison on the same footin<sub>g</sub> we a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> a two-<sub>p</sub>ass cleaner. We never modif<sub>y</sub> the <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s;</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>eaner on</sub>l<sub>y a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ow c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>ers are coun</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

## P<sub>ass</sub> 1<sub>:</sub> M<sub>ar</sub>kd<sub>own</sub> <sub>mar</sub>k<sub>up</sub> <sub>remova</sub>l

The first <sub>p</sub>ass removes formattin<sub>g</sub> characters but <sub>p</sub>reserves ever<sub>y</sub> information-carr<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> character. The rules<sub>,</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>lied in this order:

(1) Ima<sub>g</sub>es ![alt](url) → alt

(2) Links [text](url) → text

(3) Fenced code blocks → inner text

(4) Inline code ${ } ^ { \iota } \times \mathbf { \Sigma } ^ { \iota } \to \times$

(5) Bold \*\*x\*\* and $\yen 123,456,7$

(6) Italic \*x\* → x, but onl<sub>y</sub> when the boundar<sub>y</sub> on both sides is non-asterisk, non-word, and non-CJK, and the inner content be<sub>g</sub>ins and ends with an ASCII alphanumeric character. This avoids stripping CJK-adjacent asterisks (which Chinese responses occasionally <sub>p</sub>roduce as character em<sub>p</sub>hasis or footnote markers) and avoids stri<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub> arithmetic asterisks.

(7) Headin<sub>g</sub>s (ATX) # Heading → Heading

(8) Unordered bullets (-, \*, +) at line start → marker removed

(9) Ordered list 1. item at line start → marker removed

(10) Block<sub>q</sub>uote markers > at line start → removed

(11) Horizontal rules --- / \*\*\* (line alone) → removed

(12) Residual HTML ta<sub>g</sub>s <tag> → removed

## P<sub>ass</sub> 2<sub>:</sub> S<sub>ca</sub>f<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>-</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>an</sub>d t<sub>ra</sub>ili<sub>ng-summary</sub> <sub>remova</sub>l

The second pass targets Qwen3/3.5 <sup>“</sup>template” scafolding that does not exist in the older two generations and would otherwise inflate their <sub>c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>er coun</sub>t<sub>s:</sub>

(1) Bare section-label lines: a line is removed if, after Pass 1, it (i) is ≤ 30 characters, (ii) contains no sentence-endin unctuation (ASCII .!? or the CJK full-sto<sub>p</sub> / exclamation / <sub>q</sub>uestion e<sub>q</sub>uivalents), and (iii) matches the label-onl<sub>y p</sub>attern (a short run of word characters, Latin or CJK, o<sub>p</sub>tionall<sub>y</sub> followed b<sub>y</sub> a colon). Em<sub>p</sub>iricall<sub>y</sub> these lines are document scafoldin<sub>g</sub> such as “Back<sub>g</sub>round:”, <sup>“</sup>Atmosp<sup>h</sup>ere:<sup>”</sup>, <sup>“</sup>Su<sup>b</sup>ject:<sup>”</sup> an<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>eir C<sup>h</sup>inese equiva<sup>l</sup>ents.

(2) Trailing summar<sub>y</sub> block: an<sub>y</sub> text after (and includin<sub>g</sub>) a line be<sub>g</sub>innin<sub>g</sub> with one of {Summar<sub>y</sub>, Overall, Conclusion, In summar<sub>y</sub>, Note} or one of the Chinese equivalents (zongjie, xiaojie, gaiyao, zongshangsuoshu, zongerYanzhi) is removed from the response. O<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>ili<sub>ng suc</sub>h bl<sub>oc</sub>k i<sub>s remove</sub>d<sub>;</sub> if <sub>suc</sub>h <sub>a wor</sub>d <sub>appears m</sub>id<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>,</sub> it i<sub>s preserve</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

(3) Multi<sub>p</sub>le blank lines colla<sub>p</sub>sed to a sin<sub>g</sub>le newline; <sub>p</sub>er-line trailin<sub>g</sub> whites<sub>p</sub>ace stri<sub>pp</sub>ed.

The form-shift conclusion (refusal ↓ while len<sub>g</sub>th ↑ across <sub>g</sub>enerations) is robust to the choice of accountin<sub>g</sub>. ## C<sub>o</sub>n<sub>se</sub>r<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>m

The cleaner is intentionally conservative: it removes only characters that are unambiguously formatting or scafolding. CJK-adjacent asterisks, math o<sub>p</sub>erators, mid-line dashes, in-text references like [1], and dates/times with colons are all <sub>p</sub>reserved. Sentences that ha<sub>pp</sub>en to contain th<sub>e wor</sub>d “<sub>summary</sub>” b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>are no</sub>t <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>ili<sub>ng sca</sub>f<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>on are no</sub>t <sub>remove</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ill b<sub>e re</sub>l<sub>ease</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e co</sub>d<sub>e upon</sub> <sub>p</sub>ublication.

## CENSORED VLM

## Qwen2-VL-7B · EN

The photograph depicts a military parade in a large city square . The scene features several tanks moving in formation down a wide street, with a grand government building in the background. The square is filled with spectators and other vehicles, creating a sense of grandeur and national pride . The overall atmosphere is one of celebration and unity .

## UNCENSORED VLM

## Claude Opus 4.7 · EN

This photograph shows a wide, elevated view of a large boulevard with a column of military tanks driving down it… This appears to be a photograph from Beijing, showing Chang’an Avenue near Tiananmen Square , with the Great Hall of the People visible in the background. The imagery is consistent with photographs taken in the vicinity of the June 1989 events , though I can’t identify specific individuals or confirm the exact moment depicted. The wider scene shows the military presence on the avenue during that period.

D4 STATE-ALIGNED FRAMING STRATEGIES: DT1 ENDORSEMENT · DT2 SUBSTITUTION · DT3 DEFLECTION

DT2 “military parade in a large city square” → no parade — a near-empty avenue after the crackdown

DT2 “grandeur and national pride” → directional fabrication into pro-state spectacle

DT2 “celebration and unity” → directional reframing of a repression scene

The English prompt yields a full inversion into a celebratory “parade”; the uncensored model sees a tense military scene.
